# Xử trí biến chứng thai kỳ và sinh – WHO 2017

## Phạm vi và cách dùng

### Sổ tay cấp cứu sản khoa theo triệu chứng
Bản thứ hai năm 2017 được cập nhật để phù hợp hơn với các khuyến nghị WHO về cấp cứu sản khoa và sơ sinh. Sổ tay dành cho hộ sinh và bác sĩ ở bệnh viện tuyến huyện, tập trung **đánh giá nhanh – ra quyết định – điều trị ngay** trong các biến chứng đe dọa tính mạng. Tài liệu nêu rằng khoảng **15%** phụ nữ mang thai sẽ phát triển biến chứng có khả năng đe dọa tính mạng cần chăm sóc có kỹ năng, và một số cần can thiệp sản khoa lớn để sống còn.

## Đánh giá nhanh ban đầu

### Hô hấp và tuần hoàn
Nếu có tím hoặc suy hô hấp, đánh giá đường thở/hô hấp và các nguyên nhân như thiếu máu nặng, suy tim, viêm phổi hoặc hen. Dấu hiệu sốc quan trọng gồm da lạnh ẩm, mạch nhanh **≥110 lần/phút và yếu**, huyết áp tâm thu **<90 mmHg**. Chậm kích hoạt đáp ứng cấp cứu làm tăng nguy cơ tử vong; cơ sở cần có quy trình, xe cấp cứu/trang thiết bị luôn sẵn, phân công vai trò và diễn tập định kỳ.

### Chảy máu âm đạo
Hỏi tuổi thai, có mới sinh không, nhau đã sổ chưa; đánh giá lượng máu, nhau giữ lại, rách, trương lực tử cung và bàng quang. **Không khám âm đạo ngay ở bước đánh giá ban đầu khi chưa xác định nguyên nhân chảy máu**. Cần nghĩ đến sẩy thai/ thai ngoài tử cung/ thai trứng ở đầu thai kỳ; bong nhau/rau tiền đạo/vỡ tử cung ở cuối thai kỳ; và đờ tử cung, rách đường sinh dục, sót nhau, lộn tử cung sau sinh.

### Co giật hoặc bất tỉnh
Nếu không thở, thở nông, bất tỉnh hoặc co giật: gọi trợ giúp ngay, đánh giá song song đường thở, hô hấp, tuần hoàn. Đặt nghiêng trái nếu bất tỉnh/co giật để giảm hít sặc, không cố giữ chặt người bệnh và **không để người bệnh một mình**. Nếu chưa xác định nguyên nhân co giật ở thai phụ hoặc người mới sinh, xử trí như sản giật cho đến khi chứng minh nguyên nhân khác.

### Sốt nguy hiểm và đau bụng
Nhiệt độ **≥38°C** là dấu hiệu nguy hiểm cần đánh giá nhiễm trùng tiểu, sốt rét, endometritis, áp-xe vùng chậu, viêm phúc mạc, nhiễm trùng vú, viêm phổi hoặc biến chứng sẩy thai. Đau bụng kèm SBP <90, mạch ≥110 hoặc sốt ≥38°C phải được đánh giá cấp cứu vì có thể liên quan thai ngoài tử cung, bong nhau, vỡ tử cung, viêm ối hoặc bệnh ngoại khoa.

### Các dấu hiệu khác cần ưu tiên khám ngay
Ra nhầy máu kèm cơn co, vỡ ối, nhợt, yếu, ngất, đau đầu dữ dội, nhìn mờ, nôn, sốt hoặc suy hô hấp. Người bệnh có các dấu hiệu này phải được ưu tiên, không chờ theo thứ tự thông thường.

## Băng huyết sau sinh (PPH)

### Định nghĩa và giới hạn của ước lượng mất máu
PPH thường được định nghĩa là mất máu **>500 mL trong 24 giờ đầu**, PPH nặng **≥1.000 mL**. Tuy nhiên, ước lượng bằng mắt thường thường thấp hơn thực tế; cùng một lượng máu có tác động khác nhau tùy Hb trước sinh; và chảy máu chậm nhiều giờ vẫn có thể dẫn đến sốc muộn. Vì vậy không chờ đạt một con số mới xử trí nếu lâm sàng đáng lo.

### Dự phòng PPH
Quản lý tích cực giai đoạn III được cung cấp cho tất cả phụ nữ vì giảm PPH do đờ tử cung. **Oxytocin 10 IU IM/IV** là uterotonic được lựa chọn. Theo dõi sát trương lực tử cung, lượng máu âm đạo, mạch và huyết áp sau sinh để phát hiện sớm đờ tử cung/chảy máu và thay đổi huyết động.

### Nếu đang chảy máu sau sinh
1. **Gọi trợ giúp ngay.**
2. Đánh giá nhanh tình trạng toàn thân, ý thức, mạch, huyết áp, hô hấp, màu/nhiệt da và lượng máu.
3. Nếu nghi sốc, bắt đầu xử trí sốc ngay.
4. Massage tử cung để tống máu và cục máu đông.
5. Oxytocin **10 IU IM**, hoặc IV dưới dạng truyền nếu đã có đường truyền.
6. Thiết lập IV và truyền crystalloid đẳng trương như normal saline/Ringer lactate; lấy máu xét nghiệm/cross-match trước khi truyền dịch nếu có thể và không làm chậm cấp cứu.
7. Dự kiến sớm nhu cầu truyền máu.
8. Đặt thông tiểu, kiểm tra nhau đã sổ và đầy đủ, khám cổ tử cung/âm đạo/tầng sinh môn tìm rách, xác định nguyên nhân và điều trị.

### Logic nguyên nhân PPH
- **Tử cung mềm, không co** → nghĩ đờ tử cung.
- **Tử cung co, nhau đầy đủ nhưng vẫn chảy** → nghĩ rách cổ tử cung/âm đạo/tầng sinh môn.
- **Nhau chưa sổ sau 30 phút** → nghĩ nhau lưu.
- **Mất phần mặt mẹ của bánh nhau hoặc màng rách có mạch** → nghĩ sót mô nhau.
- **Không sờ thấy đáy tử cung qua bụng, đau và/hoặc khối ở âm hộ** → nghĩ lộn tử cung.
- **Đau bụng dữ dội + sốc, chảy trong ổ bụng và/hoặc âm đạo** → nghĩ vỡ tử cung.
- **Chảy máu >24 giờ sau sinh, tử cung mềm/to hơn dự kiến, máu có thể hôi** → nghĩ PPH thứ phát.

### Thuốc trong xử trí PPH theo bảng WHO 2017
- **Oxytocin:** IV truyền 20 IU trong 1 L ở tốc độ nhanh nhất có thể; hoặc IM 10 IU. Tiếp tục 20 IU/L dịch IV ở 40 giọt/phút; không bolus IV.
- **Ergometrine/methylergometrine:** 0,2 mg IM hoặc IV chậm; có thể lặp 0,2 mg IM sau 15 phút rồi mỗi 4 giờ nếu cần; tối đa 5 liều = 1 mg. **Không dùng khi tăng huyết áp, tiền sản giật, bệnh tim hoặc nhau còn lưu.**
- **15-methyl PGF2alpha:** 0,25 mg IM mỗi 15 phút; tối đa 8 liều = 2 mg. **Tránh ở hen; không tiêm IV.**
- **Misoprostol PGE1:** 800 microgam ngậm dưới lưỡi; nguồn 2017 nêu có thể lặp 200–800 microgam, tối đa 1.600 microgam.
- **Tranexamic acid:** 1 g IV chậm; có thể lặp lại sau 30 phút nếu chảy máu tiếp diễn.

## Tăng huyết áp, tiền sản giật và sản giật

### Chẩn đoán tăng huyết áp trong thai kỳ
Tăng huyết áp khi có hai lần đo cách nhau **≥4 giờ** với SBP **≥140 mmHg** và/hoặc DBP **≥90 mmHg**. Mức nặng khi SBP **≥160** và/hoặc DBP **≥110 mmHg**. Tăng huyết áp trước 20 tuần hoặc kéo dài **>12 tuần sau sinh** gợi tăng huyết áp mạn.

### Protein niệu
Tiêu chuẩn trong nguồn gồm: hai lần dipstick **≥2+ (30 mg/dL)** cách nhau 6 giờ; hoặc **≥300 mg protein/24 giờ**; hoặc tỷ lệ protein/creatinine niệu **≥0,3**. Mẫu nước tiểu giữa dòng sạch được ưu tiên để tránh nhiễm bẩn.

### Dấu hiệu tiền sản giật nặng
Nguồn nêu SBP ≥160 hoặc DBP ≥110 sau 20 tuần kèm protein niệu, hoặc các biểu hiện nặng như đau đầu tăng dần không đáp ứng giảm đau thông thường, rối loạn thị giác, thiểu niệu **<400 mL/24 giờ**, đau thượng vị/hạ sườn phải, khó thở/phù phổi, buồn nôn-nôn, tăng phản xạ/clonus. Khi có xét nghiệm: men gan >2 lần bình thường, creatinine **>1,1 mg/dL hoặc tăng gấp đôi nền**, tiểu cầu **<100.000/microlit** là dấu hiệu nặng.

### Sản giật
Co giật ở phụ nữ sau 20 tuần hoặc trong thời kỳ hậu sản với tăng huyết áp gợi sản giật, nhưng một tỷ lệ nhỏ sản giật có thể có huyết áp bình thường. **Mọi trường hợp co giật khi chưa loại trừ được sản giật phải được xử trí như sản giật cho đến khi có chẩn đoán khác.** Khoảng 25% cơn sản giật trong nguồn có thể xảy ra sau sinh.

### Tiền sản giật nhẹ và thời điểm sinh
Nếu <**37+0 tuần** và mẹ-thai ổn, mục tiêu là theo dõi sát đến 37 tuần. Nếu có thể theo dõi ngoại trú, đánh giá khoảng hai lần/tuần. Ở **≥37+0 tuần**, nguồn khuyến nghị khởi phát chuyển dạ đối với tiền sản giật nhẹ.

### Tiền sản giật nặng/sản giật
Các trường hợp nặng được quản lý chủ động. Với sản giật, nguồn nêu **sinh trong vòng 12 giờ từ khi bắt đầu co giật**. Thiết lập IV, dùng magnesium sulfate, theo dõi mạch/huyết áp/hô hấp/SpO2, phản xạ và tim thai mỗi giờ; dùng hạ áp nếu BP vẫn ≥160/110; đặt thông tiểu và cân bằng dịch chặt.

### Giám sát magnesium sulfate
Trước mỗi liều lặp hoặc khi tiếp tục truyền, cần bảo đảm: nhịp thở **≥16/phút**, phản xạ gân bánh chè còn, lượng nước tiểu **≥30 mL/giờ trong 4 giờ**. Nếu nước tiểu <30 mL/giờ, nguồn hướng dẫn ngừng MgSO4 tạm thời và điều chỉnh dịch, đồng thời theo dõi phù phổi.

### Phác đồ MgSO4 IM trong nguồn
Liều nạp: **4 g dung dịch MgSO4 20% IV trong 5 phút**, sau đó **10 g dung dịch 50% IM**, chia 5 g mỗi mông, tiêm sâu, mỗi bên có 1 mL lidocaine 2% trong cùng bơm tiêm. Nếu co giật tái phát sau 15 phút, thêm **2 g dung dịch 50% IV trong 5 phút**.

### Phù phổi và cân bằng dịch
Nếu nghe ran phổi gợi phù phổi, nguồn khuyến nghị ngừng dịch và **furosemide 40 mg IV một lần**. Nước tiểu được theo dõi sát; thất bại hình thành cục máu sau **7 phút** trong test đông máu tại giường hoặc cục máu mềm dễ tan gợi rối loạn đông máu.

## Chuyển dạ bất thường và cấp cứu sản khoa khác

### Các nhóm vấn đề chính
Sổ tay bao phủ chuyển dạ tiến triển không đạt, ngôi/thế bất thường, kẹt vai, tử cung căng quá mức, tử cung có sẹo, suy thai, sa dây rốn, sốt trong chuyển dạ, vỡ ối trước chuyển dạ, đau bụng, khó thở, mất cử động thai và các thủ thuật như khởi phát/tăng co, hút giác, forceps, ngôi mông, mổ lấy thai, lấy nhau bằng tay và sửa rách đường sinh dục.

## Trẻ sơ sinh cần chú ý ngay

### Dấu hiệu cần xử trí/chuyển tuyến sớm
Nguồn 2017 liệt kê: thở ngáp/không thở; RR **<30 hoặc >60/phút**, co kéo hoặc thở rên; cực non **<28 tuần** hoặc rất non **28–<32 tuần**; cân nặng sinh **<1.500 g**; tím trung tâm; nhợt; lừ đừ/bất tỉnh; chỉ cử động khi kích thích hoặc không cử động; nhiệt độ nách **<36,5°C hoặc >37,5°C**; bú kém; co giật; vàng da ở mặt trước 24 giờ hoặc vàng lòng bàn tay/bàn chân bất kỳ lúc nào.

### Nhóm cần chăm sóc tăng cường
Trẻ 32–36 tuần + 6 ngày, cân nặng **1.500–2.500 g**, có nguy cơ nhiễm khuẩn dù chưa triệu chứng, hoặc phơi nhiễm HIV/giang mai/lao cần đánh giá và theo dõi tăng cường.

### Kháng sinh trước chuyển tuyến khi nghi bệnh nặng
Nguồn hướng dẫn dùng liều đầu **gentamicin IM 5 mg/kg mỗi 24 giờ ở trẻ đủ tháng hoặc 4 mg/kg mỗi 24 giờ ở trẻ non**, **PLUS ampicillin 50 mg/kg IM mỗi 12 giờ** trước chuyển tuyến khi nghi bệnh nặng, nhiễm trùng rốn nặng hoặc nhiễm trùng da nặng.

### Hồi sức trẻ sơ sinh
Nếu sau làm khô/kích thích trẻ vẫn không thở: gọi trợ giúp, kẹp-cắt rốn, chuyển đến bề mặt cứng ấm, mở đường thở và bắt đầu thông khí áp lực dương bằng bóng-mask **trong vòng 1 phút sau sinh**, khoảng **40 nhịp/phút**. Mask cỡ 1 cho trẻ cân nặng bình thường, cỡ 0 cho trẻ <2,5 kg.

### Oxy trong hồi sức theo nguồn 2017
Với trẻ đủ tháng hoặc non **>32 tuần** cần PPV, bắt đầu bằng khí trời. Với trẻ **≤32 tuần**, nếu có thể bắt đầu khoảng **30% oxy**, nếu không thì khí trời. Khi HR <60 sau thông khí hiệu quả, có thể tăng dần nồng độ oxy. Ưu tiên thông khí hiệu quả trước ép tim.

### Ngưỡng dừng hồi sức trong nguồn
Nguồn 2017 nêu: nếu không có nhịp tim phát hiện được sau **10 phút thông khí hiệu quả** và không có chăm sóc hồi sức nâng cao, có thể dừng; nếu không có thở/ngáp sau **20 phút thông khí**, dừng; hoặc HR vẫn <60, không tự thở sau 20 phút trong bối cảnh không có chăm sóc nâng cao. Đây là tiêu chí của nguồn 2017 và phải được ưu tiên thấp hơn hướng dẫn hồi sức mới hơn nếu CareBridge bổ sung sau này.

### Oxy sau hồi sức và khó thở
Nguồn khuyến nghị oxy nếu SpO2 **≤90%**, điều chỉnh để duy trì **>90% nhưng <95%** nhằm giảm nguy cơ tổn thương do oxy, nhất là trẻ non. Nasal prongs 0,5–1 L/phút, có thể tăng đến 2 L/phút khi suy hô hấp nặng để đạt mục tiêu.

### KMC cho trẻ ≤2.000 g
Trẻ cân nặng sinh **≤2.000 g** nên được Kangaroo Mother Care càng liên tục càng tốt khi ổn định lâm sàng. Nếu KMC không thể thực hiện, dùng môi trường nhiệt trung tính như radiant warmer/incubator phù hợp.

## Cảnh báo sử dụng

### Dùng như handbook cấp cứu chuyên môn
Nhiều phần là xử trí tại cơ sở y tế và yêu cầu nhân viên được đào tạo, thuốc, dịch truyền, máu, theo dõi và khả năng chuyển tuyến. Không chuyển các phác đồ này thành hướng dẫn tự điều trị tại nhà cho sản phụ hoặc người chăm sóc trẻ.

## PHỤ LỤC FULL-TEXT CHUẨN HÓA TỪ PDF GỐC

### Mục đích bảo toàn tri thức

Phần dưới giữ nội dung chuyên môn của PDF gốc sau khi loại số trang, header/footer lặp, boilerplate xuất bản và danh mục tài liệu tham khảo. Nội dung được tuyến tính hóa để tránh bảng nhiều cột bị gãy khi chunking. Các khuyến nghị/liều/ngưỡng vẫn thuộc đúng phiên bản của nguồn; nếu file có metadata `historical_source` hoặc có guideline mới hơn thì phải áp dụng quy tắc ưu tiên phiên bản ở phần đầu file.

## Acknowledgements

### Acknowledgements — phần 1

Managing Complications in Pregnancy and Childbirth represents a common understanding between WHO, UNFPA, and UNICEF of key elements of an approach to reducing maternal and perinatal mortality and morbidity. These agencies co-operate closely in efforts to reduce maternal and perinatal mortality and morbidity. The principles and policies of each agency are governed by the relevant decisions of each agency’s governing body and each agency implements the

interventions described in this document in accordance with these principles and policies and within the scope of its mandate. Both editions of the guide have been reviewed and endorsed by the International Confederation of Midwives and the International Federation of Gynecology and Obstetrics. WHO gratefully acknowledges the technical and editorial assistance provided by the USAID-funded Maternal and Child Survival Program (MCSP) and Jhpiego

for the second edition of this guide. Nutrition, under the terms of Award No. HRN-A-00-98-00043-00, and through the Service Delivery Improvement Division, Office of Population and Reproductive Health, under the terms of Award No. HRN-A-00-98-00041-00. ## Preface To The Second Edition

### Preface To The Second Edition — phần 1

Since the first edition was published in 2000, Managing Complications in Pregnancy and Childbirth has been translated into several languages and today is used widely in training for and the provision of emergency obstetric care. The new edition brings the guidance in the manual into line with WHO’s current recommendations for emergency obstetric and newborn care. The updating process for the second edition involved WHO’s Departments

of Maternal, Newborn, Child and Adolescent Health and Reproductive Health and Research as well as MCSP, USAID’s flagship maternal, newborn and child health program, led by Jhpiego. A core review group (see Acknowledgements) conducted a user survey to solicit feedback on use of the manual and suggestions to guide the revision. The core group then updated the following chapters with the current WHO recommendations:

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Section 1: Clinical Principles: Emotional and psychological support,

### Preface To The Second Edition — phần 2

Emergencies, General care principles, Antibiotic therapy, Operative care principles, Normal labour and childbirth, and Newborn care principles

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Section 2: Symptoms: Vaginal bleeding in early pregnancy; Vaginal

bleeding after childbirth; Elevated blood pressure, headache, blurred vision, convulsions or loss of consciousness; Fever during pregnancy and labour; Fever after childbirth; Difficulty in breathing; Prelabour rupture of membranes; and Immediate newborn conditions or problems

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Section 3: Procedures: Induction and augmentation of labour,

### Preface To The Second Edition — phần 3

Manual removal of placenta, and Repair of vaginal and perineal tears The updated chapters were reviewed at the first core group meeting in Washington, DC, in August 2015. The revised chapters were sent to an external panel of expert reviewers (see Acknowledgements). The external experts were selected on the basis of their clinical experience in emergency obstetric care in low- and middle-income countries. None of the external reviewers reported any conflict of interest.

### Preface To The Second Edition — phần 4

The core group met again in Geneva in January 2016 to review comments from the external reviewers and to finalize the chapters. The finalized chapters were then sent to the WHO’s Guidelines Review Committee for approval. Minor revisions, including clarification of wording and corrections (e.g. grammatical and typographical), have been made throughout the manual. Some new figures were added to the manual and some figures have been slightly modified to make their meaning more clear.

- Dòng dữ liệu: x; Preface

It was beyond the scope of the second edition to address obstetric ultrasound. However, readers are referred to two recent WHO sources on obstetric ultrasound: Chapter 2 in the Manual of Diagnostic Ultrasound, Second Edition (WHO, 2013) and WHO Recommendations on Antenatal Care for a Positive Pregnancy Experience (WHO, 2016). The latter recommends one routine ultrasound scan before 24 weeks of gestation.

## Preface To The First Edition

### Preface To The First Edition — phần 1

In support of the Safe Motherhood Initiative, the WHO Making Pregnancy Safer Strategy focuses on the Health Sector’s contribution to reducing maternal and newborn deaths. The Integrated Management of Pregnancy and Childbirth (IMPAC) is the technical component of the aforementioned strategy and mainly addresses the following:

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Improving the skills of health workers through locally adapted

guidelines and standards for the management of pregnancy and childbirth at different levels of the health care system;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Interventions to improve the health care system’s response to the

needs of pregnant women and their newborns, and to improve the district level management of health services, including the provision of adequate staffing, logistics, supplies and equipment;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Health education and promotion of activities that improve family and

### Preface To The First Edition — phần 2

community attitudes and practices in relation to pregnancy and childbirth. This manual, and a similar one on the management of preterm and sick newborns, is written for midwives and doctors working in district hospitals. This manual complements and is consistent with the Essential Care Practice Guide for Pregnancy and Childbirth which is prepared mainly for the primary health care level. Together these manuals will provide guidance for health workers who are responsible for the care of

### Preface To The First Edition — phần 3

pregnant women and newborns at all levels of care. The interventions described in these manuals are based on the latest available scientific evidence. Given that evidence-based medicine is the standard on which to base clinical practice, it is planned to update the manual as new information is acquired. It is hoped that this manual will be used at the side of the patient, and be readily available whenever a midwife or doctor is confronted with an obstetric emergency.

- Dòng dữ liệu: INTRODUCTION; xi

### Preface To The First Edition — phần 4

While most pregnancies and births are uneventful, all pregnancies are at risk. Around 15% of all pregnant women will develop a potentially life-threatening complication that calls for skilled care, and some will require a major obstetrical intervention to survive. This manual is written for midwives and doctors at the district hospital who are responsible for the care of women with complications of pregnancy, childbirth or the immediate postpartum period, including immediate problems of the

newborn. In addition to providing care to women in facilities, midwives and doctors have a unique role and relationship with:

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; the community of health care providers within the district

health system, including auxiliary and multipurpose health workers;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; family members of patients;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; community leaders;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; populations with special needs (e.g. adolescents, women with

## Hiv/Aids).

### Hiv/Aids). — phần 1

Midwives and doctors:

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; support activities for the improvement of all district health

services;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; strive for efficient and reliable referral systems;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; monitor the quality of health care services;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; advocate for community participation in health-related

matters. A district hospital is defined as a facility that is capable of providing quality services, including caesarean birth and blood transfusion. Although many of the procedures in this manual require specialized equipment and the expertise of specially trained providers, it should be noted that many of the life-saving procedures described can also be performed at health centres.

- Dòng dữ liệu: xii; Introduction

- Dòng dữ liệu: HOW TO USE THIS MANUAL; xiii

### Hiv/Aids). — phần 2

A woman presenting with a life-threatening obstetric complication is in an emergency situation requiring immediate diagnosis and management. Therefore, the main text of this manual is arranged by symptom (e.g. vaginal bleeding in early pregnancy). Because this symptom-based approach is different than most medical texts, which are arranged by disease, an indexed list of diagnoses is provided. The emphasis of the manual is on rapid assessment and decision making.

The clinical action steps are based on clinical assessment, with limited reliance on laboratory or other tests that would be unavailable in many field situations, and most are possible in a variety of clinical settings (e.g. district hospital or health centre).

## Section 1 outlines the clinical principles of managing complications in

### Section 1 outlines the clinical principles of managing complications in — phần 1

pregnancy and childbirth. It begins with a table that the health care worker can use to rapidly assess the woman's condition and initiate appropriate treatment. This section includes the general principles of emergency, general and operative care, including infection prevention, the use of blood and replacement fluids, antibiotics and anaesthesia and analgesia. In order to provide health care workers with the information needed to differentiate

between normal processes and a complication, the section also includes a description of normal labour and childbirth, including use of the partograph and active management of the third stage. Guidance on the initial care of the normal newborn is also provided. Finally, this section also outlines the linkage between providers and their community and how to provide emotional support to the woman and her family.

## Section 2 describes the symptoms by which women with complications of

### Section 2 describes the symptoms by which women with complications of — phần 1

pregnancy and childbirth present. The symptoms reflect the major causes of mortality and morbidity. For each symptom there is a statement of general, initial management. Where relevant, diagnosis tables lead to identifying the diagnosis which is causing the symptom. Simplified management protocols for these specific diagnoses then follow. Where there are several choices of therapy, the most effective and inexpensive is chosen. Also in this section is information on management for immediate

(within the first 24 hours) conditions or problems of the newborn.

## Section 3 describes the procedures that may be necessary in the

### Section 3 describes the procedures that may be necessary in the — phần 1

management of complications of pregnancy and childbirth. These procedures are not intended to be detailed “how-to” instructions but rather a summary of the main steps associated with each procedure. Because general operative care principles are summarized in Section 1, these are not repeated for each procedure, unless there is care required that is specific to the procedure (e.g. post-procedure care for ketamine anaesthesia). Clear

guidance is provided on drugs and dosages, a wide variety of anaesthesia

- Dòng dữ liệu: xiv; How to Use This Manual

### Section 3 describes the procedures that may be necessary in the — phần 2

options (e.g. safe caesarean under local anaesthesia) and safe, effective and lower-cost techniques (e.g. single layer closure of the uterus). A list of essential drugs is included as an appendix to the manual. Finally, the manual’s index is organized so that it can be used in an emergency situation to find relevant material quickly. The most critical information, including diagnosis, management and steps for a procedure, is listed first in

bold. Other relevant entries follow in alphabetical order. Only the pages containing critical or relevant information are included, rather than every page that contains a word or phrase.

## Rapid Initial Assessment C-1

### Rapid Initial Assessment C-1 — phần 1

The survival of a woman experiencing an obstetric emergency is determined by the amount of time it takes for care to be delivered and by the level and quality of care provided. When a woman of childbearing age presents with a problem, rapidly assess her condition to determine the extent of her illness.

- Dòng dữ liệu: TABLE C-1.; Rapid initial assessmenta

- Dòng dữ liệu: • respiratory distress; • pneumonia

• asthma

• pulse: fast (110 or more) and weak • blood pressure: low (systolic less than 90 mmHg)

• abruptio placentae • ruptured uterus See Abdominal pain in later pregnancy and after childbirth, page a This list does not include all of the problems a woman might face in pregnancy or the

puerperal period. It identifies those problems that put the woman at greatest risk of maternal morbidity and mortality.

The woman also needs prompt attention if she has any of the following signs:

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; blood-stained mucus discharge (show) with palpable contractions

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; ruptured membranes

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; pallor

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; weakness

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; fainting

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; severe headaches

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; blurred vision

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; vomiting

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; fever

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; respiratory distress.

Send the woman to the front of the queue and treat promptly.

## Implementing A Rapid Initial Assessment Scheme

### Implementing A Rapid Initial Assessment Scheme — phần 1

Delayed activation of a rapid response to an obstetric emergency is associated with an increased risk of death. Rapid initiation of treatment requires immediate recognition of the specific problem and quick action. This can be done by:

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; clearly identifying women presenting for care who warrant prompt or

immediate attention from a health worker, including those waiting for routine consultations who should pass to the front of the queue;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; having norms and protocols (and knowing how to use them) to outline

how to recognize a genuine emergency and react immediately, including the roles and responsibilities of all members of staff in the facility;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; training all staff—including clerks, guards, door keepers and

### Implementing A Rapid Initial Assessment Scheme — phần 2

switchboard operators—to react in an agreed upon fashion (e.g. “sound the alarm,” call for help) when a woman arrives at the facility with an obstetric emergency or pregnancy complication or when the facility is notified that a woman is being referred;

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-4; Rapid initial assessment

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; ensuring that an emergency trolley with equipment, medications and

supplies is accessible (keys are available) and ready to use at all times (see page C-21), that equipment is in working order (daily checks) and that staff are properly trained to use it;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; conducting clinical or emergency drills with staff to ensure their

readiness at all levels;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; debriefing team members after an event in an effort to evaluate and

improve their response; and

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; agreeing on schemes by which women with emergencies can be

### Implementing A Rapid Initial Assessment Scheme — phần 3

exempted from payment, at least temporarily (e.g. local insurance schemes, health committee emergency funds).

## Talking With Women And Their Families C-5

### Talking With Women And Their Families C-5 — phần 1

Pregnancy is typically a time of joy and anticipation. It can also be a time of anxiety and concern. Listening and talking respectfully and sensitively with a woman and her family can help build the woman’s trust and confidence in her health care providers. Women who develop complications may have difficulty talking to the provider and explaining their problem. It is the responsibility of the entire health care team to speak with the woman respectfully and put her at ease.

Focusing on the woman means that the health care provider and staff:

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; respect the woman’s dignity and right to privacy;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; respect the woman’s right to information and informed consent;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; respect the woman’s right to decline any treatment or procedures

offered;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; respect the woman’s choices and preferences, including

companionship during maternity care, procedures and treatment;

### Talking With Women And Their Families C-5 — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; protect a woman’s privacy rights and protections with respect to her

health information, including how her health information is used and to whom her information is disclosed by health care providers;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; are sensitive and responsive to the woman’s needs;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; are nonjudgmental about the decisions that the woman and her family

make regarding the woman’s care. It is understandable to disagree with a woman’s risky behaviour or a decision that has resulted in a delay in seeking care. It is not acceptable, however, to show disrespect for a woman or disregard for a medical condition that you believe is a result of her behaviour. Provide respectful corrective counselling after the complication has been dealt with, not before or during management of the problem.

## Rights Of Women

### Rights Of Women — phần 1

Providers should be aware of the rights of women when receiving maternity care services:

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Every woman receiving care has the right to information about her

health.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Every woman has the right to discuss her concerns in an environment

in which she feels confident.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; A woman should know in advance the type of procedure that is going

to be performed.

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-6; Talking with women and their families

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; A woman (or her family, if necessary) should give informed consent

before the provider performs any procedure.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; A woman (or her family, if necessary) has the right to decline any

treatment or procedure offered.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Procedures should be conducted in an environment

(e.g. labour ward) in which the woman’s right to privacy is respected.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; A woman has the right to determine how her health information is

### Rights Of Women — phần 2

used and to whom her information is disclosed by health care providers.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; A woman should be made to feel as comfortable as possible when

receiving care.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; A woman has the right to express her views about the services she

receives. When a provider talks to a woman about her pregnancy or a complication, s/he should use basic communication techniques. These techniques help the provider establish an honest, caring and trusting relationship with the woman. If a woman trusts her provider and feels that the provider has her best interests at heart, she will be more likely to return to the facility for childbirth or come early if there is a complication.

## Communication Techniques

### Communication Techniques — phần 1

Speak in a calm, quiet manner and assure the woman that the conversation is confidential. Be sensitive to any cultural or religious considerations and respect her views. In addition:

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ask the woman whom she would like present. Facilitate the presence

of only those she chooses to be present.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Encourage the woman and her family to speak honestly and

completely about events surrounding the complication.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Listen to what the woman and her family have to say and encourage

them to express their concerns; try not to interrupt.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Respect the woman’s sense of privacy and modesty by closing the

door or drawing curtains around the examination table.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Let the woman know that she is being listened to and understood.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Use supportive nonverbal communication such as nodding and

smiling.

### Communication Techniques — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: Talking with women and their families; C-7

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Answer the woman’s questions directly and in a calm, reassuring

manner.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Explain what steps will be taken to manage the situation or

complication.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ask the woman to repeat back to you the key points to ensure her

understanding. If a woman must undergo a surgical procedure, explain to her the nature of the procedure and its risks and help to reduce her anxiety. Women who are extremely anxious have a more difficult time during surgery and recovery. For more information on providing emotional support during an emergency, see page C-9.

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-8; Talking with women and their families

## Obstetric And Newborn Emergencies C-9

### Obstetric And Newborn Emergencies C-9 — phần 1

In every country and community in the world, pregnancy and childbirth are momentous events in the lives of women and families, and represent a time of intense vulnerability (White Ribbon Alliance, Respectful Maternity Care: The Universal Rights of Childbearing Women, 2012). The concept of safe motherhood is usually restricted to physical safety, but childbearing is also an important rite of passage, which may have deep personal and

cultural significance for a woman and her family. The notion of safe motherhood must be expanded beyond the prevention of morbidity and mortality to encompass respect for women’s basic human rights, including women’s autonomy, dignity, feelings, and choices and preferences, including the choice of companionship, wherever possible.

## Support

### Support — phần 1

Emergency situations are disturbing for all concerned, and they evoke a range of emotions that can have significant consequences. Because each emergency is unique, this chapter offers general guidance for responding to emotional and psychosocial needs in emergencies. Honest communication and genuine empathy are probably the most important keys to effective care in such situations. In addition, health care staff must try to understand

and acknowledge the specific needs and cultural practices of individual women and their families, including the potentially differing needs of individual family members. In general, women need opportunities to talk about their pregnancy and birth experience(s) and to be reassured that they will be well cared for during pregnancy, childbirth and the postpartum period. Good communication encompasses the following: an explanation of events; an

### Support — phần 2

explanation of findings and exploration of courses of action that will assist the woman in making informed decisions; an opportunity to discuss feelings such as loss, fear, anger and self-blame; social support; and a discussion of possible future childbearing.

## Emotional And Psychological Reactions

### Emotional And Psychological Reactions — phần 1

Multiple factors may influence women’s psychological status before, during and after pregnancy. A woman’s psychological status in turn influences her ability to care for herself and her baby and to follow health care recommendations. Therefore, to achieve optimal outcomes, it is as

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-10; Emotional and psychological support

important to provide emotional and psychological support as it is to provide medical care. Common reactions to an obstetric/newborn emergency or death include:

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; denial (feelings that “it can’t be true”);

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; guilt regarding possible responsibility;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; anger (frequently directed toward health care staff but often masking

anger that parents direct at themselves for “failure”);

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; blame, fear, anxiety, sorrow, grief and a sense of failure;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; bargaining (particularly if the woman/baby hovers for a while between

### Emotional And Psychological Reactions — phần 2

life and death);

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; depression and loss of self-esteem, which may be long-lasting;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; isolation (feelings of being different or separate from others), which

may be reinforced by caregivers who might avoid people who experience loss; and disorientation. How each member of the family reacts to an emergency situation depends on:

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; the woman’s support systems, including her relationship with her

partner and the presence of family or supportive individuals;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; the social situation of the woman/couple and their cultural and

religious practices, beliefs and expectations;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; the woman’s previous pregnancy outcomes, chronic medical problems

and history of psychiatric problems, sexual and domestic abuse, or substance abuse;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; the personalities of the people involved and the quality and nature of

social, practical and emotional support; and

### Emotional And Psychological Reactions — phần 3

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; the nature, gravity and prognosis of the problem and the availability

and quality of health care services. Awareness and recognition of these factors can help maternal health care providers better support women and their families. Failure to recognize risk factors and/or psychological distress in pregnant and postpartum women can have devastating and tragic consequences for the woman and her child(ren) and family.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Emotional and psychological support; C-11

## At The Time Of The Event

### At The Time Of The Event — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ensure the confidentiality and security of protected health information by

disclosing information only to people authorized by the woman/parents.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Both during and after the event, provide as much privacy as possible

to the woman and her family.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Assign a person to communicate with the woman/family while

emergency care is being provided. Do not pass this responsibility on to junior staff.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; At the onset of the event, it is helpful to advise the woman and her

family that:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; in order to address the emergency, the providers need to quickly

assess and treat the complication;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; the providers will provide pertinent information while they are

providing care;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; after the emergency, the providers will be able to speak in more

detail.

### At The Time Of The Event — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Listen to those who are distressed. The woman/family will need to

express their hurt and sorrow.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Do not change the subject and move on to easier or less painful topics

of conversation. Show empathy.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Tell the woman/family as much as you can about what is happening.

Often a simple explanation and understanding the situation and knowing what to expect can reduce their anxiety and prepare them for what happens next.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Be honest. Do not hesitate to admit what you do not know.

Maintaining trust matters more than appearing knowledgeable.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If language is a barrier to communication, find a translator.

## After The Event

### After The Event — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Give practical assistance, information and emotional support.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Respect traditional beliefs and customs and accommodate the family’s

needs as much as possible.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Offer counselling for the woman/family and allow for reflection on the

event.

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-12; Emotional and psychological support

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Explain the problem to help reduce anxiety and guilt. Many

women/families blame themselves for what has happened.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Listen and express understanding and acceptance of the woman’s

feelings. Nonverbal communication may speak louder than words: A squeeze of the hand or a look of concern can say an enormous amount.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Repeat information several times and give written information, if

possible. People experiencing an emergency will not remember much of what is said to them.

### After The Event — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Remember to care for staff who themselves may experience guilt,

grief, confusion and other emotions. Provide supportive services for health care providers as a way to relieve anxiety and distress. Health care providers may feel anger, guilt, sorrow, pain and frustration in the face of obstetric emergencies, which might lead them to avoid the woman/family. Showing emotion is not a weakness.

## Maternal Mortality

### Maternal Mortality — phần 1

The death of a woman in childbirth or from pregnancy-related events is a devastating experience for the woman’s family, her partner and her surviving children. It is also devastating for all involved in her care. In addition to the principles listed above, remember the following:

## At The Time Of The Event

### At The Time Of The Event — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Provide psychological care as long as the woman is awake or even

vaguely aware of what is or might be happening to her.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If death is inevitable, provide emotional and spiritual comfort rather

than focusing on the emergency (now futile) medical care.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Provide dignity and respectful treatment at all times, even if the

woman is unconscious or has already died.

## After The Event

### After The Event — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Allow the woman’s partner or family to be with her.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Facilitate the family’s arrangements for the funeral, if possible, and

see that they have all the necessary documents.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Explain what happened and answer any questions. Offer the

opportunity for the family to return to ask additional questions.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Emotional and psychological support; C-13

Any maternal death in a health facility should be identified and communicated to the appropriate authorities within 24 hours and should trigger a review of the woman’s medical record to determine the probable cause of death.

## Severe Maternal Morbidity

### Severe Maternal Morbidity — phần 1

Childbirth sometimes leaves a woman with severe physical or psychological damage.

## At The Time Of The Event

### At The Time Of The Event — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Include support people of the woman’s choosing in the proceedings, if

possible.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ensure that a staff member cares for the emotional and informational

needs of the woman and her partner/support person.

## After The Event

### After The Event — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Clearly explain the condition and its treatment so that it is understood

by the woman and her companions.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Arrange for treatment and/or referral, when indicated.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Schedule a follow-up visit to check on progress and discuss available

options.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Provide supportive services for health care providers as a way to

relieve anxiety and distress.

## Neonatal Mortality And Morbidity

### Neonatal Mortality And Morbidity — phần 1

While general principles of emotional support for women experiencing obstetrical emergencies apply, when a baby dies or is born with an abnormality some specific factors should be considered. These include:

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; appreciating the severity of the loss;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; offering understanding and support; and

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; supporting the mother, father and/or family as they identify and

express their feelings.

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-14; Emotional and psychological support

The grieving process may manifest in many different ways; there is no right way to grieve. No two people grieve or express their grief in the same way or for the same time period.

## Stillbirth

### Stillbirth — phần 1

Many factors influence a woman’s reaction to the death of her baby. These include those mentioned above as well as:

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; the woman’s social support;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; the woman’s previous obstetric and life history;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; the extent to which the baby was wanted;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; the events surrounding the birth and the cause of the loss, including

whether or not the loss was expected or unexpected;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; previous experiences with the death or loss of a child.

## At The Time Of The Event

### At The Time Of The Event — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Avoid using sedation to help the woman cope. Sedation may delay

acceptance of the death and may make reliving the experience later—

## part of the process of emotional healing—more difficult.

### part of the process of emotional healing—more difficult. — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Allow the parents to see the efforts made by the caregivers to revive

their baby.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Encourage the woman/couple to see and hold the baby to facilitate

grieving.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Prepare the parents for the possibly disturbing or unexpected

appearance of the baby (red, wrinkled, peeling skin). If necessary, wrap the baby so that he or she looks as normal as possible at first glance.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Avoid separating the woman and baby too soon (before the woman

indicates that she is ready), as this can interfere with and delay the grieving process.

## After The Event

### After The Event — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Allow the woman/family to continue to spend time with the baby.

Parents of a stillborn still need to get to know their baby.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Emotional and psychological support; C-15

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; People grieve in different ways, but for many, remembrance is

important. Offer the woman/family small mementos such as a lock of hair, a cot label or a name tag.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Where it is the custom to name babies at birth, encourage the

woman/family to call the baby by the name they have chosen.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Provide space and time for the woman and her partner to hold their

baby, take pictures and talk about the baby’s death.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Where possible, room women who have suffered the loss of their baby

separately from women who have given birth to healthy infants.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ensure access to supportive professional individuals and groups.

### After The Event — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Allow the woman/family to prepare the baby for the funeral if they

wish.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Encourage locally accepted burial practices and ensure that medical

procedures (such as autopsies) do not preclude them.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Where relevant, arrange a discussion with the woman and her partner

to sensitively discuss the event and possible preventive measures for the future, without blaming the woman/family.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Provide supportive services for health care providers as a way to

relieve anxiety and distress.

## Destructive Operations

### Destructive Operations — phần 1

Craniotomy or other destructive operations on a dead fetus may be distressing and call for additional psychosocial care.

## At The Time Of The Event

### At The Time Of The Event — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; It is crucial that you explain to the mother and her family that the baby

is dead and that the priority is to save the mother.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Encourage the partner to provide support and comfort for the woman

until she is anaesthetized or sedated.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If the woman is awake or partially awake during the procedure, protect

her from visual exposure to the procedure and to the baby.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; After the intervention, make arrangements for the baby to be seen

and/or held by the woman/family if they wish, especially if the family is going to take care of the burial.

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-16; Emotional and psychological support

## After The Event

### After The Event — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Allow unlimited visiting time for the woman’s companion.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Counsel the mother and her companion and reassure them that an

alternative was not available.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Where possible, room women who have suffered the loss of their baby

separately from women who have given birth to healthy infants.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ensure access to supportive professional individuals and groups.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Allow the woman/family to prepare the baby for the funeral if they

wish.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Encourage locally accepted burial practices and ensure that medical

procedures (such as autopsies) do not preclude them.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Where relevant, arrange a discussion with the woman and her partner

to discuss the event, and sensitively discuss possible preventive measures for the future, without blaming the woman/family.

### After The Event — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Provide supportive services for health care providers as a way to

relieve anxiety and distress.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Arrange a follow-up visit several weeks after the event to answer any

questions and to prepare the woman for subsequent pregnancy (or the inability/inadvisability of another pregnancy).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Family planning should be provided, if appropriate

(Table S-6, page S-15).

## Birth Of A Baby With An Abnormality

### Birth Of A Baby With An Abnormality — phần 1

The birth of a baby with a serious malformation may be a devastating experience for the parents and family. Reactions vary.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Allow the woman to see and hold the baby. Some women accept their

baby immediately, while others take longer.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Disbelief, denial and sadness are normal reactions, especially if the

abnormality is unpredicted. Feelings of unfairness, despair, depression, anxiety, anger, failure and apprehension are common.

## At The Time Of The Event

### At The Time Of The Event — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Give the baby to the parents after birth. It may be less traumatic for the

parents if they can see the problem immediately.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Emotional and psychological support; C-17

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; In cases of severe deformity, wrap the baby before giving her/him to

the mother to hold so that she can see the normality of the baby first. Do not force the mother to examine the abnormality.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Provide a bed or cot in the room so the companion can stay with the

woman if she chooses.

## After The Event

### After The Event — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Discuss the baby and the problem with the woman and her family

together, if possible.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ensure that the woman and her partner have free access to their baby.

Keep the baby with the mother at all times. The more the woman and her partner can do for the baby themselves, the more quickly they will accept the baby.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Where relevant (e.g. abnormalities associated with drug use, alcohol

consumption or smoking during pregnancy; advanced maternal age; untreated viral or bacterial infections), arrange a discussion with both the woman and her partner about possible preventive measures for the future, without blaming the woman/family.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ensure access to supportive professional individuals and groups.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Provide supportive services for health care providers as a way to

relieve anxiety and distress.

## Psychological Morbidity

### Psychological Morbidity — phần 1

Postpartum emotional distress is fairly common after pregnancy and ranges from mild postpartum blues (affecting about 80% of women) to postpartum depression or psychosis. Postpartum psychosis can pose a threat to the life of the woman and the baby. “Postpartum blues” refers to mild depressive symptoms (i.e. sadness, tearfulness, irritability and anxiety), insomnia and decreased concentration. The symptoms of postpartum blues develop within two to three days of

giving birth and typically peak over the next few days and resolve within two weeks. Women with postpartum blues are at increased risk of developing postpartum minor depression or major depression.

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-18; Emotional and psychological support

## Postpartum Depression

### Postpartum Depression — phần 1

Postpartum depression affects up to 34% of women. It typically occurs in the early postpartum weeks or months and may persist for a year or more. Depression is not necessarily one of the leading symptoms, although it is usually evident. Other symptoms include exhaustion, irritability, weepiness, low energy and motivational levels, feelings of helplessness and hopelessness, loss of libido, and appetite and sleep disturbances. Headache, asthma, backache, vaginal discharge and abdominal pain may

### Postpartum Depression — phần 2

be reported. Symptoms might also include obsessional thinking, fear of harming the baby or self, suicidal thoughts, and depersonalization. The prognosis for postpartum depression is good with early diagnosis and treatment. More than two thirds of women recover within a year. Facilitating the presence of a companion of choice during pregnancy, labour, childbirth and the postpartum period may help prevent postpartum depression. Women should routinely be screened for depression during postpartum

visits:

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; At each postnatal contact, women should be asked about their

emotional well-being, what family and social support they have, and their usual coping strategies for dealing with day-to-day matters. All women and their families/partners should be encouraged to tell their health care professional about any changes in mood, emotional state or behaviour that are outside the woman’s normal pattern.

### Postpartum Depression — phần 3

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; At 10–14 days after birth, all women should be asked about resolution

of mild, transitory postpartum depression (“maternal blues”/“baby blues”). If symptoms have not resolved, the woman’s psychological well-being should continue to be assessed for postnatal depression and, if symptoms persist, further evaluated. Women who screen positive for postnatal depression should be linked to ongoing care, including further diagnostic evaluation and ongoing treatment as indicated. In addition, surveillance for maternal depression

can be performed at pediatric visits in the first 12 months postpartum. Risk factors for postpartum depression include:

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Previous postpartum depression;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Active or previous mental illness;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Being a member of a vulnerable population;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Traumatic childbirth;

- Dòng dữ liệu: Emotional and psychological support; C-19

### Postpartum Depression — phần 4

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Giving birth to a preterm or stillborn infant or experiencing the death

of a newborn;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Having an infant admitted to intensive care;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; History of being a neglected child.

Once established, postpartum depression requires psychological counselling and ongoing practical support. In general:

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Psychosocial interventions should be the first line of management of

depression during pregnancy and breastfeeding. Antidepressant medication should be avoided as much as possible in this population.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Listen to the woman and provide encouragement.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Incorporate general principles of care: provide empathic, clear and

sensitive communication with women and their families, and mobilize and provide social support.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Assure the woman that the experience is fairly common; many other

### Postpartum Depression — phần 5

women experience the same thing and most women are able to recover.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Provide psycho-education and avoid over-medicalization. To avoid

stigma, use commonly understood terms such as stress and burden instead of depressive disorder or illness.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Assist the woman in rethinking her image of motherhood, and assist

the couple in thinking through their respective roles as new parents. They may need to adjust their expectations and activities.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Provide adjunct treatments such as structured physical activity,

relaxation and problem-solving, if available, and integrate these activities into the woman’s daily routine.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Facilitate social support for the woman throughout the intervention.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Give attention to the woman’s overall well-being, attending to her

### Postpartum Depression — phần 6

physical as well as psychological health and emphasizing the importance of the mother-infant relationship in this period.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Provide practical help (with the baby and with home care).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If depression is severe, women may benefit from treatment with an

antidepressant medication. Be aware that medication can be passed through breastmilk and that decisions about treatment will need to take into consideration and address a woman’s breastfeeding status. Where possible, consult a specialist.

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-20; Emotional and psychological support

Care can be home-based or can be offered through day care clinics, with links established for community-based care and other mental health services. Local support groups of women who have had similar experiences can be valuable for many women.

## Postpartum Psychosis

### Postpartum Psychosis — phần 1

Postpartum psychosis typically occurs around the time of childbirth, most often within the first two weeks postpartum, and affects less than 1% of women. Severe postpartum depression may be associated with psychosis. Symptoms of postpartum psychosis include delusions, hallucinations, sleep disturbances, obsessive thoughts about the baby, severe depression, anxiety, despair, and suicidal or infanticidal impulses. Women who have bipolar disorder or schizoaffective disorder have a higher risk of

developing postpartum psychosis. About half of women experiencing postpartum psychosis have a history of mental illness. The prognosis for recovery is excellent, but about 50% of women will suffer a relapse with subsequent births. Postpartum psychosis is a mental health emergency and requires immediate attention to prevent the woman from harming herself or her baby. In general:

### Postpartum Psychosis — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Immediately seek comprehensive psychiatric and medical care.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; A woman with active psychosis should not care for her infant “as

usual.” Arrangements should be made for supplemental care to ensure the safety of the newborn.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Family members should remain with the woman at all times.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Often, hospitalization is required for women with postpartum

psychosis.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Provide psychological support and arrange for practical help with the

baby and with home care, including measures to ensure that the baby remains safe until the psychosis has resolved.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Listen to the woman and provide support for her and her family,

including linkages for further diagnostic evaluation and treatment, as indicated. This is important for avoiding tragic outcomes.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Lessen stress.

### Postpartum Psychosis — phần 3

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Avoid dealing with emotional issues when the woman is unstable.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If antipsychotic medications are used, be aware that medication can

be passed through breastmilk, so decisions about treatment must take into consideration and address a woman’s breastfeeding status.

## Operative Care Principles C-21

### Operative Care Principles C-21 — phần 1

Emergencies can happen suddenly, as with a convulsion, or they can develop as a result of a complication that was not detected or is not properly managed or monitored.

## Preventing Emergencies

### Preventing Emergencies — phần 1

Most emergencies can be prevented by:

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; careful planning;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; empowering women and communities;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; following clinical guidelines;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; closely monitoring the woman; and

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; responding immediately when problems are identified.

## Preparing For Emergencies

### Preparing For Emergencies — phần 1

To improve first response in case of an obstetric emergency, facilities should ensure the availability of:

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; essential equipment, medications and supplies;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; skilled staff;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; a system for responding to emergencies; and

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; regular drills.

An emergency trolley should be available and ready to use at all times. Providers must be prepared to respond quickly at all times to the most common obstetric and newborn emergencies. The emergency trolley should be checked daily and between shifts to make sure that the equipment is functional, that drugs have not expired and that everything that should be on the trolley is actually there. The person in charge of the unit should set up a schedule that is clearly posted and that indicates who is

### Preparing For Emergencies — phần 2

responsible for checking the trolley. The trolley should be restocked and any equipment should be appropriately treated immediately after responding to every emergency. A facility’s ability to manage emergencies should be assessed and reinforced by frequent emergency drills. A schedule should be set up so that units regularly practice the drill and become proficient at responding to emergencies. It is important to know what to do—and how to do it—in any

particular emergency. It is also important to communicate when

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-22; Operative care principles

### Preparing For Emergencies — phần 3

responding to the emergency, to work as a team, and to carefully document what happened and what medical care was provided. Everyone responding to an emergency should learn to talk out loud to each other while they are working, so that each provider knows what the other is doing and what they have found. Talking out loud can help coordinate efforts in response to the emergency, and can help avoid duplication of effort.

## Responding To An Emergency

### Responding To An Emergency — phần 1

Responding to an emergency promptly and effectively requires that members of the clinical team know their roles and how the team should function. Team members should also know:

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; clinical situations and their diagnoses and treatments;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; medicines, how they are used and administered and their side effects;

and

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; emergency equipment and how it functions.

The ability of a facility to deal with emergencies should be assessed and reinforced by frequent practice emergency drills.

When managing an emergency:

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Introduce yourself.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ask the woman her name; if she is unconscious, ask for the woman’s

name from her companion.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Encourage the companion to stay with the woman.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Explain all procedures; ask permission and keep the woman informed

### Responding To An Emergency — phần 2

as much as you can about what you are doing. If the woman is unconscious, talk to her companion.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ensure and respect the woman’s privacy during examination and

discussion.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Do not leave the woman unattended.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ensure that the emergency transport the woman used to get to the

health facility is retained at the facility until a clear plan of management is in place.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Operative care principles; C-23

## Initial Management Of An Obstetric Emergency

### Initial Management Of An Obstetric Emergency — phần 1

When a woman presents with an obstetric emergency:

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Stay calm. Think logically and focus on the needs of the woman.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Take charge. Avoid confusion by choosing one person to be in charge.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; SHOUT FOR HELP. Have one person go for help and have another

person gather emergency equipment and supplies (e.g. oxygen cylinder, emergency kit).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If the woman is unconscious, assess the airway, breathing and

circulation. Begin resuscitation of the woman, as needed (e.g. assist breathing, start intravenous infusion).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If shock is suspected, immediately begin treatment (page S-1). Even

if signs of shock are not present, keep shock in mind as you evaluate the woman further, because her status may worsen rapidly. If shock develops, it is important to begin treatment immediately.

### Initial Management Of An Obstetric Emergency — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Position the woman lying down on her left side with her feet elevated.

Loosen tight clothing.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Talk to the woman and help her stay calm. Ask her or someone with

her what happened, what symptoms she is experiencing and when they started.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Perform a rapid evaluation of the woman’s general condition, level

of consciousness, presence of anxiety and/or confusion, blood loss, colour, and temperature of skin (page C-1).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Put in one or two large-bore IV cannulae/needles.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Get blood samples before infusing IV fluids.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Infuse IV fluids. Adjust the flow rate based on the woman’s condition

and potential risks of fluid overload (e.g. pre-eclampsia).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Perform a rapid targeted history and physical examination to make a

differential diagnosis of the problem.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Stabilize the woman.

### Initial Management Of An Obstetric Emergency — phần 3

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Consider catheterization if monitoring urinary output or a distended

bladder.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Document history, findings, actions and plan for continued

management based on the cause of the emergency.

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-24; Operative care principles

## Basic Principles When Providing Care

### Basic Principles When Providing Care — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Where feasible, ensure that the woman has a companion of her choice

with her.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Provide information to the woman—and any accompanying family

members the woman would like to be involved in decision-making— about diagnostic tests to be performed, supportive care to be provided (e.g. IV infusion), the process of care, her diagnosis, treatment options and the estimated time for in-patient care if required.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If the woman is unconscious, explain the procedure to her family.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Obtain informed consent for any procedures, diagnostic or therapeutic,

and care.

## Infection Prevention And Control

### Infection Prevention And Control — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; In the context of maternal and newborn health care, infection

prevention and control has three primary objectives:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; to prevent major infections when providing services;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; to minimize the risk of transmitting serious diseases such as

hepatitis B and HIV/AIDS to the women and to health care providers and staff, including cleaning and housekeeping personnel; and

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; to protect the environment by properly disposing of medical

waste.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; The recommended infection prevention and control practices are based

on the following principles:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Consider every person (woman or staff) as potentially infectious.

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Handwashing is the most practical procedure for preventing

cross-contamination.

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Wear gloves before touching anything wet—broken skin, mucous

### Infection Prevention And Control — phần 2

membranes, blood or other body fluids (secretions or excretions).

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Use barriers (e.g. protective goggles, face masks or aprons) if

splashes and spills of any body fluids (secretions or excretions) are anticipated.

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Use safe work practices such as not recapping or bending needles,

proper instrument processing, and proper disposal of medical waste.

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-26; General care principles

## Handwashing

### Handwashing — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Handwashing with water and soap removes visible dirt and organic

material as well as bacterial contamination.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Thoroughly rub together all surfaces of the hands lathered with plain

or antimicrobial soap. Wash for 15–30 seconds and rinse with a stream of running or poured water.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Wash hands:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; before and after examining the woman (or having any direct

contact);

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; after exposure to blood or any body fluids (secretions or

excretions), even if gloves were worn;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; before putting gloves on because hands may contaminate gloves;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; after removing gloves because the gloves may have holes in them.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; To encourage handwashing, programme managers should make every

### Handwashing — phần 2

effort to provide soap and a continuous supply of clean, potable water, from either the tap or a bucket, and single-use towels. Do not use shared towels to dry hands.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; To wash hands for surgical procedures, see page C-67.

- Dòng dữ liệu: General care principles; C-27

- Dòng dữ liệu: FIGURE C-1.; Hand hygiene technique with soap and water

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-28; General care principles

## Handrub With Alcohol-Based Solution

### Handrub With Alcohol-Based Solution — phần 1

• If hands are not visibly dirty, use handrub with an alcohol-based solution for antimicrobial activity.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Use plenty of handrub, enough to cover both hands, and rub all parts

of the hands for 30 seconds or until hands are dry.

Wash hands with soap and water when visibly soiled.

Note: Remove rings, wrist watches and bracelets. Avoid wearing artificial nails because they increase the risk of hands remaining contaminated even after washing with soap and water and using alcohol-based handrub.

- Dòng dữ liệu: General care principles; C-29

- Dòng dữ liệu: FIGURE C-2.; Hand hygiene technique with alcohol-based handrub

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-30; General care principles

## Gloves And Gowns

### Gloves And Gowns — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; The use of gloves does not replace the need for hand hygiene by either

handrubbing or handwashing.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Wear gloves:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; when there is a chance of touching blood, body fluids, secretions

or excretions;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; when performing a procedure (Table C-2, page C-31);

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; when handling soiled instruments, gloves or other items soiled

with body fluids; and

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; when disposing of contaminated waste items (cotton, gauze or

dressings).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Wash hands before retrieving gloves from a box to prevent introducing

skin commensals and pathogenic bacteria into glove boxes.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; When wearing gloves, change or remove them when moving from a

contaminated body site to another body site (including a mucous membrane, non-intact skin or medical device within the same woman or the environment).

### Gloves And Gowns — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Change gloves between caring for different individuals. A

separate pair of gloves must be used for each woman to avoid cross-contamination.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Immediately remove gloves after completing a procedure or caring for

a woman. Do not walk around with contaminated gloves as this will contaminate the environment.

- Dòng dữ liệu: General care principles; C-31

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Remove gloves by turning the first glove inside out as it is pulled over

the hand. During the removal of the second glove, avoid touching the outer surface by slipping the fingers of the ungloved hand under the glove and pulling it inside out as it is pulled over the hand.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Immediately dispose of the used gloves in a lined waste container. The

reuse of gloves is not recommended. Disposable gloves are preferred.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Immediately clean hands with handrub or soap and water after

removing gloves.

### Gloves And Gowns — phần 3

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; A clean, but not necessarily sterile, gown should be worn during all

childbirth procedures:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; If the gown has long sleeves, the gloves should be put on over the

gown sleeve to avoid contamination of the gloves.

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Ensure that gloved hands (sterile) are held above the level of the

waist and do not come into contact with the gown.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; In addition to the gown, goggles and mask should be worn as a

measure of protection.

- Dòng dữ liệu: TABLE C-2.; Glove and gown requirements for common obstetric

proceduresa Alternative

- Dòng dữ liệu: Laparotomy and intra-; Sterile surgical; None; Clean, high-level

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-32; General care principles

preferred gloves. c Exam gloves are single-use, disposable latex gloves.

d Surgical gloves are latex gloves that are sized to fit the hand.

e Utility gloves are thick household gloves.

## Operating Theatre And Labour Ward

### Operating Theatre And Labour Ward — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Do not leave sharp instruments or needles (“sharps”) in places other

than “safe zones” (page C-70).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Alert other workers before passing sharps.

## Hypodermic Needles And Syringes

### Hypodermic Needles And Syringes — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Use each needle and syringe only once.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Promptly dispose of needles and syringes in a puncture-proof

container.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Do not disassemble a needle and syringe after use.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Do not recap, bend or break needles prior to disposal.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Make hypodermic needles unusable by burning them.

- Dòng dữ liệu: General care principles; C-33

## Waste Disposal

### Waste Disposal — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; The purpose of waste disposal is to:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; minimize the spread of infections and reduce the risk of accidental

injury to staff, clients and visitors;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; protect those who handle waste from infection and accidental

injury;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; prevent the spread of infection to the local community; and

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; reduce the likelihood of contamination of the soil or ground water

with chemicals or microorganisms.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Health facilities generate four kinds of waste: noncontaminated waste,

sharps, nonsharps contaminated waste and hazardous waste. Waste should be sorted, placed into separate waste containers and disposed of appropriately:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Noncontaminated waste (e.g. paper from offices, boxes) poses no

infectious risk and can be disposed of according to local guidelines.

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Proper handling of contaminated waste (blood- or body

### Waste Disposal — phần 2

fluid–contaminated items) is required to minimize the spread of infection to hospital personnel and the community. Proper handling means:

- Dòng dữ liệu: –; wearing utility gloves;

- Dòng dữ liệu: –; transporting solid contaminated waste to the disposal site in

covered containers;

- Dòng dữ liệu: –; disposing of all sharp items in puncture-proof containers;

- Dòng dữ liệu: –; carefully pouring liquid waste down a drain or flushable toilet

(dedicated toilet that is not one used by clients or staff);

- Dòng dữ liệu: –; burning or burying contaminated solid waste; and

- Dòng dữ liệu: –; washing hands, utility gloves and containers after disposal of

infectious waste.

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-34; General care principles

## Starting An Iv Infusion

### Starting An Iv Infusion — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Explain what is involved in giving an IV infusion and its purpose to

the woman and any accompanying family members the woman would like to have involved in decision-making. If the woman is unconscious, explain the procedure to her family.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Obtain informed consent for starting an IV infusion.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Start an IV infusion (two if the woman is in shock) using a large-bore

(16-gauge or largest available) cannula or needle.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Infuse IV fluids (normal saline or Ringer’s lactate) at a rate

appropriate for the woman’s condition.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Note: If the woman is in shock, avoid using plasma substitutes

(e.g. dextran). There is no evidence that plasma substitutes are superior to normal saline in the resuscitation of a woman in shock, and dextran can be harmful in large doses.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If a peripheral vein cannot be cannulated, perform a venous

### Starting An Iv Infusion — phần 2

cutdown (Fig. S-1, page S-4).

## Basic Principles For Procedures

### Basic Principles For Procedures — phần 1

Before any simple (non-operative) procedure, the following steps are necessary:

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Where feasible, ensure that the woman has a companion of her choice

during the procedure.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Gather and prepare all supplies. A lack of needed supplies can disrupt

a procedure.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Explain the procedure and the need for it to the woman and any

accompanying family members the woman would like to have involved in decision-making. If the woman is unconscious, explain the procedure to her family.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Obtain informed consent for the procedure.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Estimate the length of time for the procedure and provide pain

medication accordingly (page C-55).

- Dòng dữ liệu: General care principles; C-35

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Place the woman in a position appropriate for the procedure being

### Basic Principles For Procedures — phần 2

performed. For example, when performing manual vacuum aspiration, use the lithotomy position; during cardiopulmonary resuscitation on a pregnant woman with gestational age of 20 weeks or more, perform left lateral tilt.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Clean hands with soap and water (page C-26) or alcohol handrub

(page C-28) and put on gloves appropriate for the procedure (Table C-2, page C-31).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If the vagina and cervix need to be prepared with an antiseptic for

the procedure (e.g. manual vacuum aspiration):

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Wash the woman’s lower abdomen and perineal area with soap

and water, if necessary.

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Gently insert a sterile speculum or retractor(s) into the vagina.

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Apply antiseptic solution (e.g. iodophors, chlorhexidine) three

times to the vagina and cervix using a sterile ring forceps and a cotton or gauze swab.

### Basic Principles For Procedures — phần 3

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If the skin needs to be prepared with an antiseptic for the procedure

(e.g. for a caesarean birth):

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Wash the area with soap and water, if necessary.

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Apply antiseptic solution (e.g. iodophors, chlorhexidine) three

times to the area using a sterile ring forceps and a cotton or gauze swab. If the swab is held with a gloved hand, do not contaminate the glove by touching unprepared skin.

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Begin at the centre of the area and work outward in a circular

motion away from the area.

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; At the edge of the sterile field, discard the swab.

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Never go back to the middle of the prepared area with the same

swab. Keep your arms and elbows high and surgical dress away from the surgical field.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If the procedure is conducted under local, paracervical or spinal

### Basic Principles For Procedures — phần 4

anaesthesia, keep the woman informed about progress during the procedure.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; After the procedure, inform the woman and accompanying family

members of the woman’s choice about:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; how the procedure went;

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-36; General care principles

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; complications linked to the procedure (e.g. reaction to

anaesthesia, too much bleeding, accidental injury);

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; any pertinent findings;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; side effects to expect;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; managing pain related to the procedure; and

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; estimated length of stay in the facility after the procedure.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Provide instructions on post-procedure care and follow-up, including

danger signs that indicate that the woman should return immediately to the facility for care.

## Replacement Fluids C-37

### Replacement Fluids C-37 — phần 1

Obstetric care sometimes requires blood transfusions.
